<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For uniform self-avoiding walks of every sufficiently large integer length on the honeycomb lattice, we prove diameter exponent 3/4 and simultaneous local-mass and covering exponent 4/3, with arbitrary positive exponent slack and arbitrary polynomial failure probability.

- **claim-fixed-length-geometry** (theorem, [source](#stmt-09-fast-paths-fixed-length)): For every $\delta>0$ and $k>0$, there are constants $C_{\delta,k}<\infty$ and $n_0(\delta,k)$ such that, for every integer $n\ge n_0(\delta,k)$, an event of $\mathbb P_n$-probability at least $1-C_{\delta,k}n^{-k}$ has all the following properties: $$\begin{aligned} n^{3/4-\delta}&\le D(\gamma)\le n^{3/4+\delta},\label{eq:main-diameter}\\ n^{-\delta}\min\{n,s^{4/3}\} &\le M_\gamma(z,s)\le n^{\delta}\min\{n,s^{4/3}\},\label{eq:main-mass}\\ n^{-\delta}(1+ns^{-4/3}) &\le \mathcal N_\gamma(s)\le n^{\delta}(1+ns^{-4/3}).\label{eq:main-cover} \end{aligned}$$ The last two bounds hold simultaneously for every $z\in V(\gamma)$ and every real $s\in[1,n]$ (with $z$ relevant only to <a href="#eq:main-mass" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:main-mass">[eq:main-mass]</a>).
  - Explanation: Establishes simultaneous diameter, local vertex mass, and covering bounds for uniform fixed-length honeycomb walks, with arbitrary positive exponent slack and arbitrary polynomial failure probability.
- **claim-directional-span** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-directional-span)): For every $\delta,k>0$, outside an event of $\mathbb P_n$-probability $O_{\delta,k}(n^{-k})$, the projection of $V(\gamma)$ onto each unit normal to a direction of triangular-lattice edges has span at least $n^{3/4-\delta}$. This holds for every sufficiently large integer $n$.
  - Explanation: The trace has a large projection in every lattice normal direction on a simultaneous high-probability event.
- **claim-covering-dimension-ratio** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-dimension)): For each fixed $\epsilon\in(0,3/4)$, $$\sup_{1\le s\le n^{3/4-\epsilon}} \left\|\frac{\log\mathcal N_\gamma(s)}{\log(D(\gamma)/s)}-\frac43\right\| \longrightarrow0 \quad\hbox{in }\mathbb P_n\hbox{-probability}.$$ The statistic can be defined arbitrarily when $D(\gamma)\le n^{3/4-\epsilon}$.
  - Explanation: The logarithmic covering ratio converges in probability at scales polynomially separated from the diameter scale; this is a lattice covering statistic.
- **claim-gyration-and-moments** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-gyration)): Let $$\bar\gamma=\frac1{n+1}\sum_{j=0}^n\gamma_j, \qquad R_g(\gamma)^2=\frac1{n+1}\sum_{j=0}^n\|\gamma_j-\bar\gamma\|^2.$$ Then $R_g=n^{3/4+o(1)}$ with the same arbitrary polynomial probability convention as in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">[thm:main]</a>. For every fixed $p>0$, $$\mathbb E_n[D^p]=n^{3p/4+o(1)},\qquad \mathbb E_n[R_g^p]=n^{3p/4+o(1)}.$$
  - Explanation: Radius of gyration has the diameter exponent under the same probability convention, and every fixed positive moment has the corresponding exponent.
- **claim-port-extension** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-port-extension)): A weak bridge in any lattice normal direction can be extended to a strict port bridge between the first lattice boundary lines outside its height range. The height and length change by $O(1)$, the weight ratio is bounded above and below by positive constants, and the inverse operation has bounded multiplicity after recording its endpoint types.
  - Explanation: A bounded local endpoint modification converts weak bridges into strict port bridges while controlling weight distortion and inverse multiplicity.
- **claim-boundary-flux-and-strip-mass** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-01-boundary)): For every finite simply connected union $D$ of triangular tiles with simple polygonal boundary and each boundary source port $a$, $$\sum_{b\in\partial D}\sum_{\gamma:a\to b} \rho^{\|\gamma\|}e^{3iW(\gamma)/8}=1,$$ where $W$ is total signed turning from the initial inward direction to the final outward direction. If $D$ is convex, its unsigned exit mass is at most $1/c$. For each $h\ge1$, all strip sums defined above are finite and $$cA_h+B_h=1,\qquad B_{h+1}\le B_h,\qquad B_h\asymp(1+h)^{-1/4},\qquad m_h\asymp h^{3/4},\qquad m_{h+1}-m_h\asymp B_h.$$ The constants are independent of $h$, and $b_h(x)=b_h(-x)$.
  - Explanation: Supplies the boundary winding identity, convex exit bound, finite strip sums, strip balance, monotonicity, and quantitative bridge and arch estimates used by the geometric proof.
- **claim-length-nesting-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-05-nest-normalization)): For the two face centers $f_1,f_2$ adjacent to $p$, $$\frac1{\sqrt2}\bigl(Z_{f_1}(D)+Z_{f_2}(D)\bigr) \le L_p(D)\le Z_{f_1}(D)+Z_{f_2}(D).$$ The same comparison holds with finite quantities in every infinite strip of fixed positive height. Moreover, $P_2(r)=r^{1/12+o(1)}$ and $S_k=k^{1/12+o(1)}$.
  - Explanation: Compares weighted chords through a mid-edge with adjacent face nesting masses, including finite strip quantities and the diameter-truncated planar nesting exponent.
- **claim-raw-strip-first-moment** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-02-length-reduction)): From one fixed bottom port of the $h$-band strip, $$\sum_{\gamma\text{ arch or bridge}}\rho^{\|\gamma\|}\|\gamma\| \le h^{13/12+o(1)}.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the unnormalized first-length mass of arches and bridges from a fixed strip source; it is not a conditional mean.
- **claim-polygon-second-moment** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-07-magnetic-analysis-moment)): For unrooted, unoriented simple honeycomb polygons modulo triangular-lattice translations, $$\sum_{[\ell]:\,\operatorname{diam}\ell\le H} \rho^{\|\ell\|}\|\ell\|^2\le H^{2/3+o(1)}.$$
  - Explanation: Bounds the length-square mass of simple polygon translation classes, with rooting and orientation excluded from the counting convention.
- **claim-uniform-cylinder-arc** (bound, [source](#stmt-09-fast-paths-one-arc-bound)): There are constants $\delta_1>0$, $L_*>0$, and $C<\infty$ such that $A(p,p')\le b^C$ whenever $$L_Y\ge L_X\ge L_*,\qquad s\le b,\qquad L_X=\tfrac34\log s,\quad L_Y=\tfrac34\log b,$$ and at least fraction $1/2-\delta_1$ of the ordinary $X$-sites are upper. There is no positive lower bound on $L_X/L_Y$.
  - Explanation: Provides a uniform polynomial arc-mass bound even when the shorter wall remains bounded relative to a growing longer wall.
- **claim-full-box-susceptibility** (bound, [source](#stmt-09-fast-paths-full-box)): Let $S(H)$ be the maximum, over starting vertices in a box of side $H$, of the critical mass of walks confined to that box, with arbitrary endpoint and length. There are constants $C_0,C_1$ such that $S(H)\le C_0(1+H)^{C_1}$.
  - Explanation: Controls the critical mass of all lengths and endpoints in a finite box by one fixed polynomial, allowing exterior pieces to be summed freely.
- **claim-convex-localization** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-08-localization)): In a convex lattice domain, removing the part beyond a lattice cut compares the mass of old boundary exits lost with the mass of exits on the new cut, up to absolute positive constants. In particular, paths from a side which reach a parallel cut $r$ bands away have boundary-exit mass at most $C B_r$. In a strip of height $h$, the arch or bridge mass from a fixed bottom port lost by requiring lateral extent at most $M$ is at most $$C h M^{-5/4},\qquad M\ge C_1h.$$
  - Explanation: Compares exit sums across convex cuts and bounds lateral mass lost from a strip; the comparison concerns sums, not individual prefix continuation weights.
- **claim-kernel-variation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-08-variation)): With consecutive top ports identified by their tangential coordinates, $$\sum_x\|b_h(x+1)-b_h(x)\|\le C(B_h-B_{h+1}).$$
  - Explanation: Controls tangential variation of the endpoint kernel by the drop in total bridge mass between successive heights.
- **claim-seam-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-08-seam-moments)): Let $\mathcal F$ be a family of port paths, and let $N(\gamma)$ count specified seams in an interval of $R$ parallel lattice levels. They are either once-crossed levels, or twice-crossed levels with both endpoints on their near side; in the latter case the excursions beyond the counted levels are nested. Suppose that, after cutting at any ordered list of such seams, the end pieces have bounded strip or convex boundary sums when summed successively from their cut ports. Allow a factor $R_0^C$ for source and placement choices, where $C$ is independent of the number of selected seams and $R\le C_0R_0$. Then, for every fixed positive integer $j$, the weighted count over $\mathcal F$ satisfies $$\sum_\gamma\rho^{\|\gamma\|}N(\gamma)^j \le R_0^C C_j \begin{cases} R^{1+3(j-1)/4},&\text{one crossing},\\ R^{1+(j-1)/2},&\text{one arch excursion}. \end{cases}$$ Consequently counts exceeding $R^{3/4}R_0^\varepsilon$, respectively $R^{1/2}R_0^\varepsilon$, have arbitrarily polynomially small weighted mass as $R_0\to\infty$, including after multiplication by any fixed polynomial in $R_0$.
  - Explanation: High weighted seam-count moments control recovery multiplicities when the specified end-piece sums are bounded, separately for single crossings and nested arch excursions.
- **claim-fixed-endpoint-tubes** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-08-fixed-tube)): There is $v>0$ such that, for every fixed $\delta>0$, every attainable endpoint displacement $\|x\|\le vh$ has bridge mass at least $h^{-5/4-o(1)}$ in a tube of transverse radius $\delta h$ about its straight join. The estimate is uniform in the endpoint and large $h$.
  - Explanation: Provides a uniform positive mass lower bound for every attainable endpoint in the allowed slope range inside a thin tube, with exponent slack.
- **claim-free-endpoint-tubes** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-08-free-tube)): For each $\delta>0$ there are $c,P>0$ such that a height $h$ bridge, with free terminal port, has mass at least $$c h^{-1/4}(s/h)^P$$ under the requirement that its lateral deviation at height $y$ is at most $\delta(s+y)$, whenever $s\le h$ exceeds a fixed threshold. Heights along an edge may be interpolated. In particular a fixed-proportion tube about the starting normal has mass at least $c_\delta h^{-1/4}$.
  - Explanation: Free-terminal bridge mass retains the strip power in a fixed-proportion tube and pays a power of initial clearance for a widening tube.
- **claim-localized-arch-kernel** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-08-arch-kernel)): Two distinct ports on a lattice side line, at distance $r$, can be joined in either prescribed half-plane by arches of diameter at most $Cr$, of total mass at least $r^{-5/4-o(1)}$.
  - Explanation: Constructs positive localized arch mass joining distinct boundary ports in either prescribed half-plane, with controlled diameter and exponent slack.
- **claim-localized-strip-second-moment** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-08-strip-second)): For each fixed $C_0$, and a fixed starting port, $$\sum_{\substack{\gamma\text{ arch or bridge in height }h\\ \operatorname{diam}\gamma\le C_0h}} \rho^{\|\gamma\|}\|\gamma\|^2 \le h^{29/12+o(1)}. \label{eq:R-32a}$$
  - Explanation: Exterior closures and recoverable boundary lines transfer the polygon second moment to confined strip arches and bridges.
- **claim-useful-endpoint-separation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-08-endpoint-separation)): For every fixed $\eta>0$, the preceding first-length lower bound persists after retaining only chords with the following property: endpoints on the same side have separation at least $H^{1-\eta}$; endpoints on distinct sides have maximum distance from their common corner at least $H^{1-\eta}$.
  - Explanation: Removing chords with closely spaced same-side endpoints or endpoints near their common corner preserves the bulk first-length lower exponent.
- **claim-exterior-strip-connectors** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-08-connectors)): Embed a triangle of side $H=\lfloor c_0M\rfloor$, for small fixed $c_0>0$, in a strip of height $M$. For each of the endpoint types in Lemma <a href="#lem:08-endpoint-separation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:08-endpoint-separation">19</a>, choose one of the finitely many lattice strip orientations. The strip can be placed in $\asymp M$ ways with both boundaries at distance at least $cM$ from $D$. At endpoint scale $s$, two disjoint exterior connectors to free ports on these boundaries have product mass at least $$H^{-1/2-o(1)}(s/H)^P, \label{eq:R-32c}$$ for a finite fixed $P$. They and $D$ stay in a box of diameter $CM$, with $C,P$ independent of $\eta$.
  - Explanation: Routes separated triangle chord endpoints to free outer-strip ports through disjoint exterior connectors with scale-dependent positive mass.
- **claim-localized-bridge-length-seed** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-08-localized-length)): For a sufficiently large fixed $C$, the height $M$ bridges from a fixed source and of diameter at most $CM$ have first-length mass $M^{13/12-o(1)}$ in the lower-exponent sense. Consequently, for every fixed $\xi>0$, the normalized critical bridge law satisfies $$\mathbb P_M^{\mathrm{br}}\{\|\gamma\|\ge M^{4/3-\xi}\}\ge M^{-o(1)}. \label{eq:R-32d}$$
  - Explanation: Transfers central chord length into confined bridges and obtains a subpower probability of a long bridge; this seed is not yet a high-probability estimate.
- **claim-sharp-obstacle-corridor** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-08-sharp-corridor)): Fix $D<\infty$ and $\delta>0$. There is $H_0=H_0(D,\delta)$ such that the following holds for $H\ge H_0$. Let $a=(x,u)$ be a horizontal boundary port and let $0\le v-u\le H$. Suppose a set of forbidden vertices and edges in the slab $u\le y\le v$ is contained in $\{x'\le x+DH\}$ and its part in $u\le y\le\min(v,u+\delta H)$ is contained in $\{x'\le x-\delta H\}$. The mass of strict bridges from $a$ to a free port on $y=v$, avoiding this set, is at least $$c_{D,\delta}H^{-1/4}.$$ The bridges can be confined to a box of diameter $C_{D,\delta}H$. The empty bridge is used when $v=u$. The reflected assertion also holds.
  - Explanation: Sews a finite chain of normal tubes and turns around an obstacle while retaining sharp free-terminal mass without exponent loss.
- **claim-shallow-long-arch** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-08-long-arch)): For every fixed $\sigma>0$ and all sufficiently large fixed $D'$, there are $c,C>0$ and $h_0$ with the following property for $h\ge h_0$. From any horizontal boundary port $a=(x_A,u)$, there is arch mass between $ch^{-1/4}$ and $Ch^{-1/4}$ which stays in $$u<y<u+\sigma h,\qquad x>x_A-\sigma h,$$ except at its two boundary endpoints, and has free terminal port on $y=u$ with abscissa exceeding $x_A+D'h$. The arches can be confined to a box of diameter $Ch$.
  - Explanation: Builds arches with large terminal displacement in a shallow strip and bounds their mass above and below by the sharp free-end strip power.
- **claim-renewal-trial** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-08-renewal-trial)): For $x\ge2l\ge2$, $$\mathbb P\{U_{T(l)}>x\}\le C(l/x)^{3/4}.$$ For every fixed $\xi>0$, a trial beginning at a renewal and ending at the first renewal more than $h$ bands above it has probability at least $h^{-o(1)}$ of containing $h^{4/3-\xi}$ vertices.
  - Explanation: Controls renewal overshoots and shows that a fresh renewal trial has a subpower chance of containing a long prefix.
- **claim-typical-finite-bridge** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-08-bridge-length)): For every $\varepsilon>0$ there is $c_\varepsilon>0$ such that a critical height-$H$ bridge satisfies $$\mathbb P_H^{\mathrm{br}}\{H^{4/3-\varepsilon}\le\|\gamma\| \le H^{4/3+\varepsilon}\} \ge1-O_\varepsilon(H^{-c_\varepsilon}).$$ Its diameter is at least $d_0H$ and at most $CH^{1+\varepsilon}$ with polynomially small exceptional probability.
  - Explanation: Independent trials and exact renewal conditioning give polynomial concentration of finite-bridge length and a polynomially likely diameter upper bound.
- **claim-jump-charges-and-renewals** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-10-charges)): For an irreducible bridge let $J$ be its height and $W$ its maximum absolute horizontal displacement from its start. For dyadic $a\ge1$ and real $d\ge0$, $$\mathbf P\{a\le J<2a,\ W\ge da\} \le Ca^{-\alpha}\Phi(d),\qquad \Phi(d)=(1+d)^{-5/4}.$$ Moreover, for $b\ge0$ and $x\ge1$, $$\mathbf E\sum_{k:U_{k-1}<b} \min\{1,(J_k+W_k)/x\} \le C(1+b)^\alpha x^{-\alpha}.$$ For every positive integer $p$, the number of renewals in any height interval of length $v\ge0$, after a stopping renewal, has $p$-th moment at most $C_p(1+v)^{p\alpha}$, uniformly in the stopping history and interval location.
  - Explanation: Bounds irreducible height-width tails, stopped accumulated charges including the overshooting jump, and renewal-count moments after stopping renewals.
- **claim-ordered-probes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-10-ordered-probes)): The constants $L,L_2,K_1,K_2,K_3$ can be chosen so that, for all integer $1\le h,h_1,h_2\le r$, with $r$ dyadic and $d\ge0$, $$\mathbf P(E(h))\le K_1h^{-\alpha},\qquad F(h,r,d)\le K_2r^{-\alpha}\Phi(d),\qquad F_{12}(h_1,h_2,r)\le K_3r^{-\alpha}.\label{eq:T-34}$$ Either adjacent-port choice and each reflected orientation obey the same bounds.
  - Explanation: Controls ordered adjacent renewal tests and marked giant integrals, including simultaneous inspection of both ends of a shared irreducible whose weight enters once.
- **claim-hairpin-occurrence-masses** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-10-hairpins)): For fixed qualification parameters and all sufficiently large scales, with $r$ dyadic, $1\le h,h_1,h_2\le r$, and $d\ge0$, $$Y(h)\le Ch^\alpha,\qquad X_d(h,r)\le Ch^\alpha r^{-\alpha}\Phi(d),\qquad X_{12}(h_1,h_2,r)\le Ch_1^\alpha h_2^\alpha r^{-\alpha}.$$ Bounded scales can be included by changing constants.
  - Explanation: Recoverable hairpin sewing bounds qualified renewal-occurrence masses, with a shared giant charged once in the paired construction.
- **claim-usable-renewals** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-10-usable-renewals)): Stop a worker at its first renewal at or above $s$, where $s/h$ is a sufficiently small fixed constant. Call this history regular when the sum of $J+W$, including its last jump, is less than $h/16$. For every $\delta>0$ one can choose fixed qualification parameters so that, for all sufficiently large $h$ depending on these choices, conditionally on any such history and any fixed opposite path, with failure probability at most $\delta$, continued $E(h)$ implies at least $c h^\alpha$ qualified worker renewals in $[eh,2h/5]$. For two workers with regular histories, outside total conditional failure probability at most $2\delta$, continued $E(h)$ supplies at least $ch^{2\alpha}$ separated renewal pairs. In each pair the lower renewal is a qualified worker against the opposite prefix ending at the higher renewal, whose height exceeds it by at least $2\sigma h$. The failure probabilities are taken before conditioning on continued $E(h)$.
  - Explanation: A regular stopped history yields many qualified renewals whenever order continues, except for a conditional failure event measured before conditioning on continued order.
- **claim-logarithmic-first-hit-avoidance** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-10-log-first-hit)): For every integer $h\ge1$, the first-hit probability just defined satisfies $$S_2(h)\le Ch^{-\alpha}(1+\log h)^2.\label{eq:T-35a}$$
  - Explanation: Transfers ordered renewal-stop control to disjoint first-hit paths by summing overshooting jump birth dyads, leaving a logarithmic loss.
- **claim-adjacent-arm-power-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-09-fast-paths-adjacent-arms)): For every $\epsilon>0$, the probability $S_2(H)$ of disjoint first-hit paths from neighboring ports satisfies $S_2(H)\le C_\epsilon H^{-3/4+\epsilon}$.
  - Explanation: Absorbs the logarithmic first-hit loss into arbitrary positive exponent slack.
- **claim-integrated-short-bridge-deficit** (bound, [source](#stmt-09-fast-paths-integrated-short)): For every sufficiently small $\varepsilon>0$, there are $\kappa,a_0>0$ such that, for each fixed $C<\infty$, $$\sum_{0\le j\le Cr}B_j[\,\|\gamma\|\le2H^{4/3-\varepsilon}\,] \le C' r^{3/4}H^{-a_0}, \qquad H^{1-\kappa}\le r\le CH. \label{eq:09-short-gain}$$
  - Explanation: An integrated height sum of length-capped bridges gains a power deficit uniformly over the stated near-macroscopic scale range.
- **claim-turning-extremum-decomposition** (construction, [source](#stmt-09-fast-paths-turns)): Fix $d\ge1$ and put $k=2^d$. A weak up bridge of height in $[H,2H]$ admits an averaged decomposition into $k$ weak up pieces, each of span in $[c_dH,2H]$, separated by $k-1$ retreats. If the retreat spans have dyadic scales $s_\ell$, with scale 1 including empty and bounded retreats, the total level-choice weight of a fixed decomposition is at most $C_d\prod_\ell(s_\ell/H)$.* *Every piece can be recoded as a strict port bridge, with bounded weight change and finitely many local inverse records per cut. It gains only $O(1)$ length. At a turn whose retreat span grows as a positive power of $H$, the two departing arms start at neighboring ports and remain disjoint through first visits to any common distance $o(s_\ell)$ and $o(H)$ into their shared side.
  - Explanation: An averaged extremal decomposition produces macroscopic forward pieces and retreats with recoverable local recodings and preserved neighboring first-hit arms.
- **claim-marked-terminal-avoidance** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-09-fast-paths-marked-conditioning)): Consider two independent renewal end strings from neighboring ports. For either string use one of the following measures: its probability law stopped at the first renewal above $q$, or its bridge measure summed over a marked terminal renewal with $f\le1+U<2f$, where $f\ge1$. Call the first kind good and the second kind marked. A good end has mass scale 1 and probe scale $q$; a marked end has mass scale $f^{3/4}$ and probe scale $f$. Requiring the two strings to avoid through their first visits to a small fixed multiple of the smaller probe scale costs at most $S_2(t)$, times a constant and the two mass scales. Here $t$ is that small multiple, rounded to an integer; bounded scales use the bound 1. The assertion remains valid at all turns of a chain simultaneously when different turns use distinct end strings and all other incidence constraints are dropped.
  - Explanation: Stopped and terminally marked end strings retain avoidance costs after conditional integration; simultaneous multiplication requires distinct strings at different turns.
- **claim-fast-bridge-amplification** (bound, [source](#stmt-09-fast-paths-fast-bridges)): For every $\varepsilon>0$ and every $A<\infty$, $$\sum_{H\le h\le2H} B_h[\,\|\gamma\|\le H^{4/3-\varepsilon}\,] \le C_{\varepsilon,A}H^{-A}. \label{eq:F-33b}$$
  - Explanation: A sufficiently large fixed number of recoverable turns amplifies the short-length deficit to arbitrary polynomial decay of fast-bridge mass.
- **claim-uniform-local-length** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-11-local-length)): For every $\tau>0$ and $A>0$ there is $C_{\tau,A}<\infty$ such that, for every $H\ge2$, outside a set of $\mu_H$-mass at most $C_{\tau,A}H^{-A}$, every vertex subwalk $\sigma$ satisfies $$\|\sigma\|\le H^\tau(1+s(\sigma))^{4/3}, \qquad s(\sigma)=\text{its vertical span in band units}. \label{eq:L-37}$$ The same conclusion holds for each of the finitely many lattice normal directions simultaneously.
  - Explanation: Every vertex subwalk obeys the length-span bound simultaneously in lattice normal directions outside an exceptional unnormalized full-box mass of arbitrary polynomial decay.
- **claim-small-irreducible-returns** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-11-small-return)): For every $c>0$ and $P>0$ there are $D<\infty$ and $C<\infty$ such that if $I$ is sampled from the irreducible-renewal law and $J$ is its height, then for every $r\ge2$, $$\mathbb P\!\left\{\begin{array}{l} J\in[r,2r),\ \text{$I$ returns to depth at most} \ r(1+\log r)^{-D}\\ \text{after first reaching depth }cr \end{array}\right\} \le C r^{-\alpha}(1+\log r)^{-P}. \label{eq:T-36}$$ The statement holds in either orientation of $I$.
  - Explanation: Deep returns inside a macroscopic irreducible bridge have an arbitrary logarithmic saving beyond its height-tail mass, in either orientation.
- **claim-thin-excursion-suppression** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-11-thin-excursions)): For every $\lambda>0$ and $A>0$, there is $C_{\lambda,A}<\infty$ such that $$\mu_H\!\left\{\begin{array}{l} \text{some subarc has vertical span at most }H^{\lambda/4}\\ \text{and length greater than }H^\lambda \end{array}\right\} \le C_{\lambda,A}H^{-A},\qquad H\ge2.$$ Subarcs may have vertex or horizontal-port endpoints, with bounded endpoint changes in their lengths and spans.
  - Explanation: Long subarcs confined to a thin vertical range have arbitrarily small polynomial full-box mass; bounded vertex-to-port changes are permitted.
- **claim-turning-chain-witnesses** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-11-chain)): There are absolute $b>0$ and $C<\infty$ such that, for fixed $\epsilon,p,U$ and a root-span dyad $s\in[r,2r)$ with $H^\beta/2\le r\le C_0H$, the weighted witness mass just defined is at most $$H^{C+o_{p,U,\epsilon}(1)}H^{-\epsilon m_*} \bigl[C'(1+\log\log H)^{C'}\bigr]^k(k+1)^{-bk}. \label{eq:L-39}$$ There $m_*=0$ for a spine and $m_*=p$ for a marked witness. The exponent $C$ is independent of $p,U,\epsilon$; the constant $C'$ may depend on them. The $o(1)$ is uniform in the indicated $r,k$, and denotes a quantity tending to zero for those fixed parameters. The same bound, with $m_*=0$ and $k$ equal to the number of pieces, holds for an alternating chain of $1\le k\le pL$ pieces of nonincreasing spans in $[r,2r)$, where $L=\lceil\log H\rceil$ and $p$ remains fixed. For this last assertion the mass is the analogous sum over all rooted chain records, without trial probabilities.
  - Explanation: Bounds trial-tree spine and marked witnesses with an expansion-dependent saving, and also treats comparable nonincreasing alternating chains without trial probabilities.
- **claim-many-slab-bridges** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-11-many-bridges)): Fix $D<\infty$. Consider ordered tuples of $a$ mutually avoiding strict bridges across the same $j\ge1$ bands, all contained in one abscissa slab of width $Dj$. Prescribe the distinct starting ports, the distinct ending ports, and their matching. The critical mass of these tuples is at most $$\exp\!\left(C_D\log(2+j)-\frac{c_Da^2}{\log(2+j)}\right). \label{eq:L-41}$$
  - Explanation: Reflection and concatenation convert prescribed mutually avoiding slab crossings into separating cylinder polygons and yield a quadratic penalty in the crossing count.
- **claim-uniform-local-vertex-mass** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-11-local-mass)): For every $\tau>0$ and $A>0$, there is $C_{\tau,A}<\infty$ such that for all $H\ge2$, outside a set of $\mu_H$-mass at most $C_{\tau,A}H^{-A}$, simultaneously for every visited vertex $v$ and every real radius $1\le s\le H$, $$\#\bigl(V(\gamma)\cap\overline B(v,s)\bigr)\le H^\tau s^{4/3}. \label{eq:L-42}$$
  - Explanation: Controls all visits to each ball, including separate returns, by combining the subwalk length bound with suppression of many disjoint slab crossings.
- **claim-uniform-temporal-modulus** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-11-modulus)): For every $\tau>0$ and $A>0$ there is $C_{\tau,A}<\infty$ such that, for every $H\ge2$, outside a set of $\mu_H$-mass at most $C_{\tau,A}H^{-A}$, every subwalk $\sigma$ of $l$ steps satisfies $$\operatorname{diam}(\sigma)\le H^\tau(1+l)^{3/4}.$$
  - Explanation: Every subwalk has controlled diameter in terms of its step count outside a simultaneous exceptional full-box mass of arbitrary polynomial decay.
- **claim-every-length-normalization** (bound, [source](#stmt-09-fast-paths-normalization)): For every integer $n\ge0$, $$\label{eq:normalization} Z_n\ge1.$$
  - Explanation: Submultiplicativity and polynomial strip mass rule out a critical partition function below unity at any integer length, enabling exact-length probability transfer.
- **claim-obstacle-sewing-with-slack** (construction, [source](#stmt-09-fast-paths-obstacle)): Fix $D<\infty$ and $\delta>0$. A port with abscissa $x$ on level $u$ is to be joined to level $v$, where $0\le v-u\le H$. Suppose the obstacle in this slab lies to the left of $x+DH$, and in its first $\min(\delta H,v-u)$ bands lies to the left of $x-\delta H$. The mass of bridges to a free endpoint at level $v$ avoiding that obstacle is at least $H^{-1/4-o(1)}$, uniformly over these data. The reflected statement also holds.
  - Explanation: An alternative high-moment recovery argument sews free-terminal bridges past obstacles with arbitrary exponent slack and supports asynchronous avoidance.
- **claim-irreducible-displacement-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-09-fast-paths-jump-tail)): For $x\ge1$, $$\mathbb P(W>x)\le Cx^{-3/4},\qquad \mathbb E\min\{1,(J+W)/x\}\le Cx^{-3/4}.$$
  - Explanation: Controls horizontal excursions of an irreducible bridge and the truncated height-plus-width expectation needed for stopping-history charges.
- **claim-asynchronous-adjacent-avoidance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-asynchronous-avoidance)): For every $\varepsilon>0$, $S_2(H)\le C_\varepsilon H^{-3/4+\varepsilon}$. Equivalently, $$S_2(H)\le H^{-3/4+o(1)}. \label{eq:asynchronous-avoidance}$$
  - Explanation: An independent induction extends asynchronously chosen renewal prefixes to common heights and obtains the adjacent first-hit avoidance exponent without a common original renewal level.

      "exact_statement": "<span id=\"thm:main\" label=\"thm:main\"></span>For every $\\delta>0$ and $k>0$, there are constants $C_{\\delta,k}<\\infty$ and $n_0(\\delta,k)$ such that, for every integer $n\\ge n_0(\\delta,k)$, an event of $\\mathbb P_n$-probability at least $1-C_{\\delta,k}n^{-k}$ has all the following properties: $$\\begin{aligned}\n n^{3/4-\\delta}&\\le D(\\gamma)\\le n^{3/4+\\delta},\\label{eq:main-diameter}\\\\\n n^{-\\delta}\\min\\{n,s^{4/3}\\}\n &\\le M_\\gamma(z,s)\\le n^{\\delta}\\min\\{n,s^{4/3}\\},\\label{eq:main-mass}\\\\\n n^{-\\delta}(1+ns^{-4/3})\n &\\le \\mathcal N_\\gamma(s)\\le n^{\\delta}(1+ns^{-4/3}).\\label{eq:main-cover}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The last two bounds hold simultaneously for every $z\\in V(\\gamma)$ and every real $s\\in[1,n]$ (with $z$ relevant only to <a href=\"#eq:main-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:main-mass\">[eq:main-mass]</a>).",
      "explanation": "Establishes simultaneous diameter, local vertex mass, and covering bounds for uniform fixed-length honeycomb walks, with arbitrary positive exponent slack and arbitrary polynomial failure probability.",

        "exact_quote": "<span id=\"thm:01-strip\" label=\"thm:01-strip\"></span>For every finite simply connected union $D$ of triangular tiles with simple polygonal boundary and each boundary source port $a$, $$\\sum_{b\\in\\partial D}\\sum_{\\gamma:a\\to b}\n       \\rho^{|\\gamma|}e^{3iW(\\gamma)/8}=1,$$ where $W$ is total signed turning from the initial inward direction to the final outward direction.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-08-localization",
        "exact_quote": "In a convex lattice domain, removing the part beyond a lattice cut compares the mass of old boundary exits lost with the mass of exits on the new cut, up to absolute positive constants. In particular, paths from a side which reach a parallel cut $r$ bands away have boundary-exit mass at most $C B_r$. In a strip of height $h$, the arch or bridge mass from a fixed bottom port lost by requiring lateral extent at most $M$ is at most $$C h M^{-5/4},\\qquad M\\ge C_1h.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-237.4"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-08-seam-moments",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal F$ be a family of port paths, and let $N(\\gamma)$ count specified seams in an interval of $R$ parallel lattice levels. They are either once-crossed levels, or twice-crossed levels with both endpoints on their near side; in the latter case the excursions beyond the counted levels are nested. Suppose that, after cutting at any ordered list of such seams, the end pieces have bounded strip or convex boundary sums when summed successively from their cut ports. Allow a factor $R_0^C$ for source and placement choices, where $C$ is independent of the number of selected seams and $R\\le C_0R_0$. Then, for every fixed positive integer $j$, the weighted count over $\\mathcal F$ satisfies $$\\sum_\\gamma\\rho^{|\\gamma|}N(\\gamma)^j\n \\le R_0^C C_j\n \\begin{cases}\n R^{1+3(j-1)/4},&\\text{one crossing},\\\\\n R^{1+(j-1)/2},&\\text{one arch excursion}.\n \\end{cases}$$ Consequently counts exceeding $R^{3/4}R_0^\\varepsilon$, respectively $R^{1/2}R_0^\\varepsilon$, have arbitrarily polynomially small weighted mass as $R_0\\to\\infty$, including after multiplication by any fixed polynomial in $R_0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.4"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Fix $D<\\infty$ and $\\delta>0$. There is $H_0=H_0(D,\\delta)$ such that the following holds for $H\\ge H_0$. Let $a=(x,u)$ be a horizontal boundary port and let $0\\le v-u\\le H$. Suppose a set of forbidden vertices and edges in the slab $u\\le y\\le v$ is contained in $\\{x'\\le x+DH\\}$ and its part in $u\\le y\\le\\min(v,u+\\delta H)$ is contained in $\\{x'\\le x-\\delta H\\}$. The mass of strict bridges from $a$ to a free port on $y=v$, avoiding this set, is at least $$c_{D,\\delta}H^{-1/4}.$$ The bridges can be confined to a box of diameter $C_{D,\\delta}H$. The empty bridge is used when $v=u$. The reflected assertion also holds.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.4"
      },

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-08-sharp-corridor",

      "exact_statement": "Fix $D<\\infty$ and $\\delta>0$. There is $H_0=H_0(D,\\delta)$ such that the following holds for $H\\ge H_0$.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-10-usable-renewals",
        "exact_quote": "Stop a worker at its first renewal at or above $s$, where $s/h$ is a sufficiently small fixed constant. For every $\\delta>0$ one can choose fixed qualification parameters so that, for all sufficiently large $h$ depending on these choices, conditionally on any such history and any fixed opposite path, with failure probability at most $\\delta$, continued $E(h)$ implies at least $c h^\\alpha$ qualified worker renewals in $[eh,2h/5]$. For two workers with regular histories, outside total conditional failure probability at most $2\\delta$, continued $E(h)$ supplies at least $ch^{2\\alpha}$ separated renewal pairs.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-09-fast-paths-turns",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $d\\ge1$ and put $k=2^d$. A weak up bridge of height in $[H,2H]$ admits an averaged decomposition into $k$ weak up pieces, each of span in $[c_dH,2H]$, separated by $k-1$ retreats. If the retreat spans have dyadic scales $s_\\ell$, with scale 1 including empty and bounded retreats, the total level-choice weight of a fixed decomposition is at most $C_d\\prod_\\ell(s_\\ell/H)$.*\n\n*Every piece can be recoded as a strict port bridge, with bounded weight change and finitely many local inverse records per cut. It gains only $O(1)$ length. At a turn whose retreat span grows as a positive power of $H$, the two departing arms start at neighboring ports and remain disjoint through first visits to any common distance $o(s_\\ell)$ and $o(H)$ into their shared side.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.4"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-09-fast-paths-marked-conditioning",
        "exact_quote": "Consider two independent renewal end strings from neighboring ports. For either string use one of the following measures: its probability law stopped at the first renewal above $q$, or its bridge measure summed over a marked terminal renewal with $f\\le1+U<2f$, where $f\\ge1$. Call the first kind good and the second kind marked. A good end has mass scale 1 and probe scale $q$; a marked end has mass scale $f^{3/4}$ and probe scale $f$. Requiring the two strings to avoid through their first visits to a small fixed multiple of the smaller probe scale costs at most $S_2(t)$, times a constant and the two mass scales. Here $t$ is that small multiple, rounded to an integer; bounded scales use the bound 1. The assertion remains valid at all turns of a chain simultaneously when different turns use distinct end strings and all other incidence constraints are dropped.",
        "source_id": "PM-237.4"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-11-small-return",

        "exact_quote": "There are absolute $b>0$ and $C<\\infty$ such that, for fixed $\\epsilon,p,U$ and a root-span dyad $s\\in[r,2r)$ with $H^\\beta/2\\le r\\le C_0H$, the weighted witness mass just defined is at most $$H^{C+o_{p,U,\\epsilon}(1)}H^{-\\epsilon m_*}\n \\bigl[C'(1+\\log\\log H)^{C'}\\bigr]^k(k+1)^{-bk}.\n \\label{eq:L-39}$$ There $m_*=0$ for a spine and $m_*=p$ for a marked witness. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **honeycomb lattice** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Let $\mathbb H$ be the planar dual of the equilateral triangular tiling with triangle side length one.
- **fixed-length self-avoiding walk ensemble** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Let $\mathcal W_n$ be the set of paths $$\gamma=(\gamma_0,\ldots,\gamma_n),\qquad \gamma_0=o,$$ whose consecutive vertices are adjacent in $\mathbb H$ and whose vertices are all distinct.
- **uniform fixed-length law** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Write $c_n=\|\mathcal W_n\|$, and let $\mathbb P_n$ be the uniform law on $\mathcal W_n$.
- **trace diameter and local mass** ([source](#pm-h-3)): We use the Euclidean embedding and set $$V(\gamma)=\{\gamma_0,\ldots,\gamma_n\},\quad D(\gamma)=\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}V(\gamma),\quad M_\gamma(z,s)=\|V(\gamma)\cap\overline B(z,s)\|.$$
- **ball covering number** ([source](#pm-h-3)): Let $\mathcal N_\gamma(s)$ be the least number of closed Euclidean balls of radius $s$ that cover $V(\gamma)$; their centers may lie anywhere in the plane.
- **critical mass** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A *mass* is a sum of these weights before normalization.
- **port** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A port is the midpoint of an edge of the triangular tiling.
- **port path** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A port path follows honeycomb edges, with an initial and a final half-edge, and visits each dual vertex at most once.
- **strict bridge** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A strict bridge of height $h$ goes from a bottom port to a top port of the open $h$-band strip and has no other visit to the boundary.
- **arch** ([source](#pm-h-7)): An arch has two distinct ports on the same boundary.
- **bridge endpoint kernel and total mass** ([source](#pm-h-7)): Write $$b_h(x)=\sum_{\gamma:0\to(x,h)}w(\gamma),\qquad B_h=\sum_xb_h(x),\qquad B_0=1.$$
- **normalized critical bridge law** ([source](#pm-h-7)): We write $\mathbb P_h^{\rm br}$ for the probability measure obtained by dividing these bridge weights by $B_h$.
- **weak bridge** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A weak bridge is a vertex path whose endpoints attain its minimum and maximum heights; intermediate visits to these levels are permitted.
- **polygon nest** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A polygon nest around a face center $f$ is a finite set of pairwise vertex-disjoint simple polygons, each strictly enclosing $f$.
- **irreducible bridge** ([source](#sec:renewal)): A bridge is *irreducible* if it has positive height and no once-crossed intermediate level.
- **stopped renewal-prefix law** ([source](#pm-h-20)): For every real threshold $t\ge0$, let $\mathcal Q_t$ be the law of its prefix through the first renewal at or above $t$.
- **renewal-occurrence measure** ([source](#pm-h-20)): In contrast, define the renewal-occurrence measure $\mathcal R$ on finite strings by $$\int G(P)\,d\mathcal R(P) =\mathbf E\sum_{k\ge0}G(I_1\cdots I_k).$$
- **full-box critical measure** ([source](#sec:11-slow-covering)): For a fixed lattice vertex $o$ and fixed $C_0<\infty$, write $$\mu_H(\mathcal A)= \sum_{\substack{\gamma\text{ starts at }o\\ \operatorname{diam}(\gamma)\le C_0H}} \rho^{\|\gamma\|}\mathbf 1_{\mathcal A}(\gamma),\qquad H\ge2.$$
- **additive length** ([source](#pm-h-32)): Their *additive length* counts a full edge as one and a half-edge as one half, so lengths add exactly at every cut.
- **critical fixed-length partition function** ([source](#pm-h-37)): Let $Z_n=c_n\rho^n$ be the critical partition function of the $n$-edge paths from the fixed root.

      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "normalized critical bridge law",
      "scope": "paper-wide"
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "pm-h-7",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-7",
          "exact_quote": "A weak bridge is a vertex path whose endpoints attain its minimum and maximum heights; intermediate visits to these levels are permitted.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "A weak bridge is a vertex path whose endpoints attain its minimum and maximum heights; intermediate visits to these levels are permitted.",
      "interpretation": "Endpoints attain height extremes while intermediate boundary contacts remain allowed.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "weak bridge",
      "scope": "paper-wide"
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "pm-h-8",
      "evidence": [

          "exact_quote": "A polygon nest around a face center $f$ is a finite set of pairwise vertex-disjoint simple polygons, each strictly enclosing $f$.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "A polygon nest around a face center $f$ is a finite set of pairwise vertex-disjoint simple polygons, each strictly enclosing $f$.",
      "interpretation": "Nesting configurations contain pairwise vertex-disjoint enclosing polygons.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "polygon nest",
      "scope": "paper-wide"
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "sec:renewal",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "sec:renewal",
          "exact_quote": "A bridge is *irreducible* if it has positive height and no once-crossed intermediate level.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "A bridge is *irreducible* if it has positive height and no once-crossed intermediate level.",
      "interpretation": "Cutting at every intermediate single crossing yields the unique renewal decomposition.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "irreducible bridge",
      "scope": "paper-wide"
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "pm-h-20",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-20",
          "exact_quote": "For every real threshold $t\\ge0$, let $\\mathcal Q_t$ be the law of its prefix through the first renewal at or above $t$.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "For every real threshold $t\\ge0$, let $\\mathcal Q_t$ be the law of its prefix through the first renewal at or above $t$.",
      "interpretation": "This is a probability measure on prefixes stopped at a renewal threshold.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "stopped renewal-prefix law",
      "scope": "Ordered renewal probes."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "pm-h-20",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-20",
          "exact_quote": "In contrast, define the renewal-occurrence measure $\\mathcal R$ on finite strings by $$\\int G(P)\\,d\\mathcal R(P)\n   =\\mathbf E\\sum_{k\\ge0}G(I_1\\cdots I_k).$$",
          "source_id": "PM-237.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "In contrast, define the renewal-occurrence measure $\\mathcal R$ on finite strings by $$\\int G(P)\\,d\\mathcal R(P)\n   =\\mathbf E\\sum_{k\\ge0}G(I_1\\cdots I_k).$$",
      "interpretation": "The measure counts finite renewal prefixes with their unnormalized critical weights, so endpoint occurrences are not stopped-path probabilities.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "renewal-occurrence measure",
      "scope": "Ordered probes and marked-terminal integration."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "sec:11-slow-covering",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "sec:11-slow-covering",
          "exact_quote": "For a fixed lattice vertex $o$ and fixed $C_0<\\infty$, write $$\\mu_H(\\mathcal A)=\n \\sum_{\\substack{\\gamma\\text{ starts at }o\\\\\n                  \\operatorname{diam}(\\gamma)\\le C_0H}}\n       \\rho^{|\\gamma|}\\mathbf 1_{\\mathcal A}(\\gamma),\\qquad H\\ge2.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-237.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "For a fixed lattice vertex $o$ and fixed $C_0<\\infty$, write $$\\mu_H(\\mathcal A)=\n \\sum_{\\substack{\\gamma\\text{ starts at }o\\\\\n                  \\operatorname{diam}(\\gamma)\\le C_0H}}\n       \\rho^{|\\gamma|}\\mathbf 1_{\\mathcal A}(\\gamma),\\qquad H\\ge2.$$",
      "interpretation": "The measure sums every length from a fixed vertex root under a diameter restriction and keeps the edge-count weight convention.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "full-box critical measure",
      "scope": "Uniform local length, local mass, and temporal modulus."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "pm-h-32",

        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-32",
          "exact_quote": "Their *additive length* counts a full edge as one and a half-edge as one half, so lengths add exactly at every cut.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "Their *additive length* counts a full edge as one and a half-edge as one half, so lengths add exactly at every cut.",
      "interpretation": "Full and half-edge contributions make length exactly additive in trial-tree decompositions.",
      "kind": "definition",
      "name": "additive length",
      "scope": "Turning-chain witness construction."
    },
    {
      "definition_anchor": "pm-h-37",

          "exact_quote": "Let $Z_n=c_n\\rho^n$ be the critical partition function of the $n$-edge paths from the fixed root.",
          "source_id": "PM-237.4"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "Let $Z_n=c_n\\rho^n$ be the critical partition function of the $n$-edge paths from the fixed root.",
      "interpretation": "This partition function converts exceptional critical mass at a fixed length into uniform-walk probability.",
      "kind": "notation",
      "name": "critical fixed-length partition function",
      "scope": "Exact-length transfer."
    }
  ],
  "generation_status": "COMPLETE",

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-critical-activity | critical activity | = | $(2+\sqrt2)^{-1/2}$ | Critical weights for vertex-rooted walks and port paths. |
| q-band-height | Euclidean height of a horizontal band | = | $\sqrt3/2$ | Successive horizontal tiling-edge lines. |
| q-flux-cosine | boundary flux cosine factor | = | $\cos(3\pi/8)$ | Convex exit mass and strip balance. |
| q-main-diameter-upper | fixed-length trace diameter | ≤ | $n^{3/4+\delta}$ | Arbitrary positive slack and polynomial failure exponent. |
| q-main-diameter-lower | fixed-length trace diameter | ≥ | $n^{3/4-\delta}$ | Large integer lengths with arbitrary positive exponent slack. |
| q-main-local-mass-upper | visited-vertex count in a closed ball | ≤ | $n^{\delta}\min\{n,s^{4/3}\}$ | Every visited center and every real radius in the stated interval. |
| q-main-local-mass-lower | visited-vertex count in a closed ball | ≥ | $n^{-\delta}\min\{n,s^{4/3}\}$ | Visited centers and real radii in the theorem's range. |
| q-main-cover-upper | least number of covering balls | ≤ | $n^{\delta}(1+ns^{-4/3})$ | Every real radius in the theorem's range. |
| q-main-cover-lower | least number of covering balls | ≥ | $n^{-\delta}(1+ns^{-4/3})$ | All allowed real radii, with covering centers anywhere in the plane. |
| q-strip-first-length | raw strip first-length mass | ≤ | $h^{13/12+o(1)}$ | Height-dependent bound with the stated exponent-slack interpretation. |
| q-polygon-length-square | polygon length-square mass | ≤ | $H^{2/3+o(1)}$ | Diameter-truncated polygon family. |
| q-lateral-localization-loss | strip mass lost under lateral confinement | ≤ | $C h M^{-5/4}$ | Lateral cutoff at least the stated fixed multiple of strip height. |
| q-kernel-total-variation | tangential total variation of the bridge endpoint kernel | ≤ | $C(B_h-B_{h+1})$ | Adjacent attainable top ports. |
| q-fixed-tube-mass | fixed-terminal thin-tube bridge mass | ≥ | $h^{-5/4-o(1)}$ | Large height, uniformly over attainable endpoints in the allowed slope range. |
| q-free-widening-tube-mass | free-terminal widening-tube bridge mass | ≥ | $c h^{-1/4}(s/h)^P$ | Lateral deviation bounded by the stated widening clearance. |
| q-localized-strip-second-length | confined strip second-length mass | ≤ | $h^{29/12+o(1)}$ | Fixed confinement geometry and asymptotic height. |
| q-localized-first-length-lower | confined bridge first-length mass | ≥ | $M^{13/12-o(1)}$ | Lower-exponent interpretation, not a limiting prefactor. |
| q-sharp-corridor-mass | obstacle-avoiding free-terminal strict bridge mass | ≥ | $c_{D,\delta}H^{-1/4}$ | Uniform over allowed source ports, terminal levels, and obstacle sets. |
| q-renewal-overshoot | renewal overshoot probability | ≤ | $C(l/x)^{3/4}$ | First renewal strictly beyond the threshold. |
| q-width-tail-factor | lateral excursion decay factor | = | $(1+d)^{-5/4}$ | Nonnegative width-to-height parameter. |
| q-ordered-test-probability | ordered adjacent renewal-test probability | ≤ | $K_1h^{-\alpha}$ | Uniform over the stated integer test heights and reflected orientations. |
| q-paired-giant-probe-mass | paired ordered-probe integral with a shared giant | ≤ | $K_3r^{-\alpha}$ | Both test heights within the giant's dyadic scale. |
| q-first-hit-logarithmic-bound | adjacent first-hit avoidance probability | ≤ | $Ch^{-\alpha}(1+\log h)^2$ | Respective first hits rather than renewal stops. |
| q-fast-bridge-mass | summed mass of fast bridges | ≤ | $C_{\varepsilon,A}H^{-A}$ | Any fixed exponent slack and any fixed failure power. |
| q-local-subwalk-length | vertex subwalk edge count | ≤ | $H^\tau(1+s(\sigma))^{4/3}$ | Simultaneously in every lattice normal direction. |
| q-many-bridges-mass | critical mass of prescribed mutually avoiding slab crossings | ≤ | $$$\exp\!\left(C_D\log(2+j)-\frac{c_Da^2}{\log(2+j)}\right). \label{eq:L-41}$$$ | The stated crossing count and positive band height. |
| q-local-vertex-mass | visited vertices in a closed ball | ≤ | $H^\tau s^{4/3}$ | Every visited center and allowed real radius simultaneously. |
| q-temporal-modulus | subwalk diameter | ≤ | $H^\tau(1+l)^{3/4}$ | Every subwalk simultaneously. |
| q-fixed-length-partition-lower | critical fixed-length partition function | ≥ | $1$ | Every integer length, including the empty walk. |
| q-irreducible-horizontal-tail | irreducible horizontal displacement tail probability | ≤ | $Cx^{-3/4}$ | Maximum absolute horizontal displacement from the starting port. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-port-extension** [claim](#stmt-lem-port-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-port-extension): Append bounded endpoint patterns outside the original height range and record their types for recovery. -> Strict port bridge with bounded distortion and inverse multiplicity.
- **step-strip-analytic-input** [claim](#stmt-lem-01-boundary) / [proof](#sec:inputs): Use the stated companion boundary-flux and quantitative strip estimates as analytic inputs. -> Finite strip sums, convex exit control, balance and strip power estimates.
- **step-nesting-analytic-input** [claim](#stmt-thm-05-nest-normalization) / [proof](#sec:inputs): Use the stated length-nest comparison and diameter-truncated nesting estimates. -> Bulk chord length lower mass through finite-domain nesting.
- **step-strip-first-moment-input** [claim](#stmt-prop-02-length-reduction) / [proof](#sec:inputs): Retain the unnormalized free-end arch-and-bridge first-length estimate. -> Raw strip first-length upper mass.
- **step-polygon-second-moment-input** [claim](#stmt-thm-07-magnetic-analysis-moment) / [proof](#sec:inputs): Use the marked-polygon bound with its unoriented translation-class convention. -> Polygon length-square mass upper bound.
- **step-one-arc-input** [claim](#stmt-09-fast-paths-one-arc-bound) / [proof](#sec:inputs): Use the uniform physical cylinder arc estimate over its asymmetric wall domain. -> A polynomial arc-mass bound valid for unequal wall scales.
- **step-full-box** [claim](#stmt-09-fast-paths-full-box) / [proof](#proof-stmt-09-fast-paths-full-box): Separate suffix-test failures, construct an injective cylinder row for successful arcs, bound unsuccessful prefixes, and absorb a small multiple of box mass. -> Polynomial susceptibility for every length and endpoint in a box.
- **step-convex-localization** [claim](#stmt-lem-08-localization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-08-localization): Subtract real winding identities across convex cuts and average side-exit loss over translated sources. -> Convex exit comparison and lateral localization loss.
- **step-kernel-variation** [claim](#stmt-lem-08-variation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-08-variation): Add a local rhombus bump, subtract imaginary winding identities, and sum disjoint new-arch families. -> Tangential kernel variation bounded by bridge-mass decrement.
- **step-seam-moments** [claim](#stmt-lem-08-seam-moments) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-08-seam-moments): Order selected seams, drop mutual avoidance, sum bridge-gap factors, and apply high-moment thresholds. -> Weighted recovery-count moments and polynomially small high-multiplicity exceptions.
- **step-fixed-tubes** [claim](#stmt-lem-08-fixed-tube) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-08-fixed-tube): Use good kernel heights, symmetric self-convolution, recovered sewing levels, and finite tube-radius iteration. -> Uniform thin-tube mass for attainable prescribed endpoints.
- **step-free-tubes** [claim](#stmt-lem-08-free-tube) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-08-free-tube): Sum small-displacement endpoint pairs and compare representation first and second moments over separated seam intervals. -> Sharp free-terminal tube mass and widening-clearance penalty.
- **step-localized-arch** [claim](#stmt-lem-08-arch-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-08-arch-kernel): Retain a macroscopic arch-displacement window, attach disjoint prescribed tubes, and recover the varied joining line. -> Localized half-plane arch kernel.
- **step-localized-strip-second** [claim](#stmt-prop-08-strip-second) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-08-strip-second): Close arches and bridges outside their strip, recover coupled boundary lines, and compare with polygon length-square mass. -> Confined strip second-length upper mass.
- **step-chord-endpoint-separation** [claim](#stmt-lem-08-endpoint-separation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-08-endpoint-separation): Close excluded chords at their endpoint scales, bound squared-length mass and inverse placements, and compare their first-length contribution with bulk nesting. -> Separated chords retaining the bulk first-length exponent.
- **step-exterior-connectors** [claim](#stmt-lem-08-connectors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-08-connectors): Fit disjoint widening tubes outside the triangle, use convex cuts at target strip lines, and remove switching-seam multiplicity. -> Exterior connectors with controlled scale penalty and diameter.
- **step-localized-length-seed** [claim](#stmt-prop-08-localized-length) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-08-localized-length): Attach connectors over strip placements, control inverse triangle positions, and apply weighted Cauchy–Schwarz to localized length moments. -> Confined first-length lower exponent and subpower long-bridge probability.
- **step-sharp-corridor** [claim](#stmt-lem-08-sharp-corridor) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-08-sharp-corridor): Fit separated normal tubes and turning regions, order recovery cuts, and use representation second moments to preserve the sharp mass power. -> Obstacle-avoiding free-terminal corridors without exponent loss.
- **step-shallow-long-arch** [claim](#stmt-lem-08-long-arch) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-08-long-arch): Turn a separated normal chain back to its starting boundary and use lateral localization for the upper bound. -> Sharp long-arch mass in a shallow strip.
- **step-renewal-trial** [claim](#stmt-lem-08-renewal-trial) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-08-renewal-trial): Use unique irreducible factorization to normalize the renewal law, sum overshoot tails, and compare renewal-count moments. -> Overshoot control and a successful fresh trial with subpower probability.
- **step-typical-finite-bridge** [claim](#stmt-thm-08-bridge-length) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-08-bridge-length): Amplify independent renewal trials, transfer through an exact terminal-height density, and use first-moment and lateral-tail upper bounds. -> Polynomially typical finite-bridge length and diameter control.
- **step-jump-charges** [claim](#stmt-lem-10-charges) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-10-charges): Prepend and append freely summed bridges to marked jumps, use lateral loss, and restart after ordered renewal occurrences. -> Height-width charges and stopped renewal-count moments.
- **step-hairpin-masses** [claim](#stmt-lem-10-hairpins) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-10-hairpins): Attach long arches and continuations, compare paired representations, retain a central shared giant, and close dyadic mass inductions. -> Qualified single and paired endpoint-occurrence mass bounds.
- **step-usable-renewals** [claim](#stmt-lem-10-usable-renewals) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-10-usable-renewals): Control renewal concentration and lateral charges, freeze the opposite path, and discard endpoints near its slab extremum using kernel variation. -> Many qualified renewals and separated pairs with failure measured before continued-order conditioning.
- **step-ordered-probes** [claim](#stmt-thm-10-ordered-probes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-10-usable-renewals-2): Induct jointly on smaller ordered tests and marked giant integrals, peel irregular histories through dyadic charges, then count usable renewal choices. -> Single-test, giant-tail, and shared-giant paired probe bounds.
- **step-log-first-hit** [claim](#stmt-cor-10-log-first-hit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-10-log-first-hit): Convert first-hit disjointness to lower ordered tests and sum dyadic births of jumps overshooting the target. -> Adjacent first-hit avoidance bound with logarithmic loss.
- **step-adjacent-arm-power** [claim](#stmt-09-fast-paths-adjacent-arms) / [proof](#proof-stmt-09-fast-paths-adjacent-arms): Absorb the logarithmic loss into arbitrary positive exponent slack. -> Adjacent-arm avoidance power bound.
- **step-integrated-short-deficit** [claim](#stmt-09-fast-paths-integrated-short) / [proof](#proof-stmt-09-fast-paths-integrated-short): Split the height sum below and above a near-macroscopic threshold and use typical bridge lower-length failure for the latter. -> Uniform integrated short-bridge mass saving.
- **step-turning-decomposition** [claim](#stmt-09-fast-paths-turns) / [proof](#proof-stmt-09-fast-paths-turns): Average middle-third cut levels, recursively retain turning extrema, and record bounded neighboring-port recodings. -> Recoverable forward pieces and retreats with level-choice weights.
- **step-marked-end-integration** [claim](#stmt-09-fast-paths-marked-conditioning) / [proof](#proof-stmt-09-fast-paths-marked-conditioning): Expose threshold renewal stops and bound remaining terminal-occurrence sums uniformly, using disjoint relative end variables across turns. -> Valid avoidance costs for stopped and marked ends.
- **step-fast-bridges** [claim](#stmt-09-fast-paths-fast-bridges) / [proof](#proof-stmt-09-fast-paths-fast-bridges): Partition every piece into disjoint or shared-jump end strings, combine length and avoidance gains, and choose a sufficiently large fixed turn count. -> Arbitrary polynomial suppression of fast-bridge mass.
- **step-small-returns** [claim](#stmt-lem-11-small-return) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-11-small-return): Decompose a deep return into comparable pieces, integrate residual height matching before probe laws, and balance logarithmic cutoffs. -> Arbitrary logarithmic saving for deep irreducible returns.
- **step-thin-excursions** [claim](#stmt-lem-11-thin-excursions) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-11-thin-excursions): Use lattice area to force an oblique span, replace the length deficit by restricted endpoint offsets, and apply turning amplification. -> Arbitrary polynomial suppression of long thin subarcs.
- **step-chain-witnesses** [claim](#stmt-lem-11-chain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-11-chain): Partition end variables into disjoint probe blocks, suppress early-return dyads, integrate translated middle-height windows, and count close-height sibling pairs. -> Spine, marked-tree, and comparable-chain witness bounds.
- **step-local-length** [claim](#stmt-thm-11-local-length) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-11-chain-2): Suppress spine and marked witnesses, apply the deterministic no-witness induction, project away trial variables, and sum decreasing span dyads. -> Uniform subwalk length-span bounds in all lattice normal directions.
- **step-many-slab-bridges** [claim](#stmt-lem-11-many-bridges) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-11-many-bridges): Reflect alternating prescribed tuples across successive strip boundaries, close each strand through a cylinder period, and recover inputs at fixed cuts. -> Quadratic crossing-count mass penalty in a slab.
- **step-local-vertex-mass** [claim](#stmt-prop-11-local-mass) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-11-local-mass): Divide ball visits into bounded-span batches and extract many disjoint face crossings when accumulated mass is excessive. -> Simultaneous local vertex-mass upper bound including repeated returns.
- **step-temporal-modulus** [claim](#stmt-prop-11-modulus) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-11-modulus): Extract a directional weak bridge from a diameter violation, extend its endpoints, and apply fast-bridge suppression after summing exterior pieces. -> Uniform subwalk diameter bound by step count.
- **step-every-length-normalization** [claim](#stmt-09-fast-paths-normalization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-09-fast-paths-normalization): Assume a partition function below unity, use submultiplicativity to obtain exponential decay, and contradict the polynomial strip lower bound. -> Partition-function lower bound at every integer length.
- **step-fixed-length-main** [claim](#stmt-09-fast-paths-fixed-length) / [proof](#proof-stmt-09-fast-paths-normalization-2): Divide exceptional fixed-length mass by its partition function, apply slow and fast bounds to the whole walk, fit contiguous blocks into balls, and recenter covering balls. -> Simultaneous fixed-length diameter, local-mass, and covering estimates.
- **step-directional-span** [claim](#stmt-cor-directional-span) / [proof](#proof-stmt-09-fast-paths-normalization-3): Apply the simultaneous directional local-length bound to the entire fixed-length walk and convert band spans to Euclidean projections. -> Lower span in each lattice normal.
- **step-covering-dimension** [claim](#stmt-cor-dimension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-09-fast-paths-normalization-4): Take logarithms of the diameter and covering bounds while keeping the scale denominator polynomially separated. -> Uniform logarithmic covering-ratio convergence in probability.
- **step-gyration-moments** [claim](#stmt-cor-gyration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-09-fast-paths-normalization-5): Use the pair-distance identity and local-mass upper bound for gyration, then bound bad-event moments using deterministic linear size and arbitrary failure powers. -> Gyration exponent and positive diameter and gyration moments.
- **step-obstacle-with-slack** [claim](#stmt-09-fast-paths-obstacle) / [proof](#proof-stmt-09-fast-paths-obstacle): Fit a separated normal-direction corridor, vary its joints, and discard high recovery counts using fixed seam moments. -> Alternative obstacle-avoiding free-terminal bridge mass with exponent slack.
- **step-displacement-tail** [claim](#stmt-09-fast-paths-jump-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-09-fast-paths-jump-tail): Count marked large-width renewal jumps, stop after detection, use fresh continuation occurrences and lateral loss, then integrate the resulting tail. -> Horizontal displacement and truncated combined-charge bounds.
- **step-asynchronous-avoidance** [claim](#stmt-thm-asynchronous-avoidance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-asynchronous-avoidance): Induct on first-hit survival, retain many separated asynchronous renewals, sew shorter prefixes to common heights, and control inverse seams using telescoping strip mass. -> Independent adjacent-arm avoidance power estimate.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-uniform-fixed-length-trace**: Diameter, local vertex mass, and covering bounds hold on one simultaneous event under the uniform fixed-length law, with positive exponent slack and arbitrary polynomial failure decay.
- **invariant-local-mass-controls-separate-visits**: The local mass bound controls all visited vertices in a ball, including vertices belonging to separate visits; it requires the repeated-crossing argument in addition to the subwalk length bound.
- **invariant-shared-irreducible-weight**: The paired ordered-probe integral uses the same irreducible bridge from its opposite ends. Its critical weight enters once, while the prefix and companion-path integrations separate conditional on its shape.
- **invariant-partition-normalization-every-length**: The critical partition function is bounded below at every integer length. Dividing an absolute exceptional critical mass by that partition function preserves its polynomial decay at the specified length.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-use-deterministic-small-length-sizing**: Treat the asymptotic exponents as deterministic sizing rules at a selected small length. -> The statement permits exceptional walks and supplies existence of constants and a sufficiently large length threshold rather than numerical tolerances for an arbitrary selected length.
- **anti-use-endpoint-separation-from-diameter**: Infer a lower bound on endpoint separation from the lower bound on trace diameter. -> A spatially extended trace can return close to its starting point.
- **anti-use-subwalk-length-as-total-ball-mass**: Use the subwalk length estimate alone to bound every vertex visited inside a ball. -> The walk may leave the ball and return through separate subwalks, whose combined mass is not controlled by a bound on an individual subwalk.
- **anti-use-independent-costs-for-shared-jump**: Multiply independent giant-jump probabilities when opposite-end probes inspect a shared irreducible bridge. -> The inspections share one shape and one critical weight; replacing it with independent copies changes the integral being bounded.
- **anti-use-continuum-or-lattice-universality**: Promote the lattice covering exponent to a continuum Hausdorff dimension or transfer it to another lattice. -> Neither a continuum scaling limit nor a universality theorem is established by the stated geometric conclusions.
- **anti-use-usability-after-survival-conditioning**: Apply the usable-renewal failure probability after conditioning the future paths on continued avoidance. -> Conditioning on continued avoidance can bias the fresh future law used to obtain the failure bound.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The selected engineering guarantees concern asymptotic probabilities and unnormalized critical masses over unrestricted path lengths. The fixed-length theorem asserts existence of constants and a sufficiently large length threshold; finite enumeration cannot decide these quantified scaling conclusions. The source explicitly states: "In particular, the notation $n^{a+o(1)}$ below always has an explicitly stated probability interpretation; it does not assert a limiting multiplicative constant." Although the proof contains local geometric constructions, they do not supply a finite executable certificate for the simultaneous diameter, local-mass, and covering assertions.

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-lattice-and-uniform-ensemble** (hypothesis): The fixed-length conclusions require the regular honeycomb lattice and the uniform law on rooted self-avoiding walks with a free endpoint. Blocked use: ['Transfer the exponents to another lattice without a separate theorem.', 'Apply the probability bounds to biased, interacting, or self-intersecting path ensembles.']
- **limit-asymptotic-threshold-and-slack** (approximation): The main theorem provides existential constants and a sufficiently large length threshold depending on positive exponent slack and the requested polynomial failure power. Blocked use: ['Assign calibrated tolerances at a selected finite length from the exponents alone.', 'Remove exponent slack or treat the bounds as deterministic guarantees.']
- **limit-local-centers-and-radius-range** (hypothesis): The simultaneous local-mass bounds use visited vertices as centers and only the real radii in the stated range; covering balls have unrestricted centers. Blocked use: ['Apply the local lower bound at an arbitrary unvisited center.', 'Extend the simultaneous radius range without another argument.']
- **limit-trace-versus-endpoints** (formal_scope): Diameter and gyration describe the whole trace. Their lower bounds do not supply a lower bound on the distance between the terminal and initial vertices. Blocked use: ['Replace trace diameter by end-to-end displacement.', 'Use gyration moments as endpoint-displacement moments.']
- **limit-discrete-covering-versus-continuum** (formal_scope): The covering-dimension conclusion is a lattice statistic in probability at polynomially separated scales. A lattice scaling limit is neither assumed nor proved. Blocked use: ['Claim convergence to a continuum curve.', 'Identify a continuum Hausdorff dimension from the discrete covering ratio.', 'Claim universality when changing lattice.']
- **limit-critical-weight-and-length-conventions** (hypothesis): The geometric proof uses critical weights with edge counts for vertex-rooted walks and visited-vertex counts for port paths. These conventions must be preserved when cutting and normalizing. Blocked use: ['Substitute another activity in the strip identities.', 'Treat edge-count and port vertex-count weights as identical without accounting for endpoint changes.']
- **limit-mass-is-not-probability** (formal_scope): A critical mass is unnormalized. Bridge probabilities require their height-specific denominator, while fixed-length probabilities require the fixed-length partition function. Blocked use: ['Read a raw first-length mass as a conditional mean.', 'Read an exceptional full-box mass directly as a normalized probability.']
- **limit-fixed-source-free-endpoint** (hypothesis): A free endpoint sums over actual terminal ports from a fixed source. It does not add an independent translation sum. Blocked use: ['Add another horizontal-root or translation factor when sewing free-terminal pieces.', 'Replace the endpoint sum by an independently chosen spatial placement.']
- **limit-strict-sewing-and-endpoint-records** (hypothesis): Exact product weights require strict bridges in adjacent disjoint open strips. Weak vertex bridges need bounded endpoint modifications and records controlling inverse multiplicity. Blocked use: ['Concatenate arbitrary weak bridges while asserting exact product weights.', 'Discard endpoint-type records when bounding inverse images.']
- **limit-companion-analytic-inputs** (formal_scope): The geometric argument states analytic strip, nesting, polygon, and cylinder estimates whose complete analytic proofs belong to companion articles. Blocked use: ['Treat this paper alone as a complete derivation of all analytic inputs.', 'Replace the stated analytic inputs by historical predictions or weaker qualitative estimates.']
- **limit-cylinder-arc-physical-regime** (hypothesis): The uniform arc bound retains its lower wall-length threshold, ordering of wall scales, site-count restriction, and required upper-site fraction even though no positive wall-ratio lower bound is imposed. Blocked use: ['Apply the polynomial arc bound to arbitrary cylinder parameters.', 'Drop the upper-site fraction or minimum shorter-wall scale.']
- **limit-convex-cut-comparison** (hypothesis): Cut comparison concerns boundary-exit sums in convex domains. It does not give a uniform lower continuation weight for every individual prefix. Blocked use: ['Use the convex comparison on an arbitrary nonconvex domain.', 'Infer an individual-prefix continuation bound from an aggregate exit comparison.']
- **limit-recoverable-seam-moments** (hypothesis): Seam moment bounds require specified recoverable crossings, nested excursions in the arch case, and bounded sequential end-piece sums with placement costs independent of the number of selected seams. Blocked use: ['Apply the moment bound to arbitrary crossing lines.', 'Declare a sewing line typical without checking its end-piece sums.', 'Ignore inverse representation multiplicity.']
- **limit-fixed-versus-free-terminal-tubes** (hypothesis): Prescribed-terminal tubes require attainable displacements in the permitted slope range and allow exponent slack. Free-terminal widening tubes require initial clearance above a fixed threshold and provide a different mass bound. Blocked use: ['Use the prescribed-terminal estimate outside its attainable slope range.', 'Substitute fixed-terminal mass for the sharp free-terminal mass required in hairpin sewing.', 'Apply the widening-tube assertion below its initial-clearance threshold without the separate bounded-scale construction.']
- **limit-localized-moments-and-counting** (hypothesis): The second-length estimate requires proportional diameter confinement and a fixed source. Its polygon input counts unrooted, unoriented translation classes, with recovery multiplicities accounted for by the closure argument. Blocked use: ['Remove diameter confinement from the strip second moment.', 'Add an unrestricted polygon-root sum to the translation-class bound.', 'Ignore recovery-line multiplicity when closing a path.']
- **limit-localized-seed-needs-amplification** (approximation): The localized moment argument yields a subpower lower probability for a long bridge. It alone does not give a high-probability lower-length bound. Blocked use: ['Read the localized seed estimate as concentration.', 'Omit independent renewal trials and terminal-height conditioning when claiming typical finite-bridge length.']
- **limit-corridor-obstacle-clearance** (hypothesis): Obstacle-avoiding corridor mass requires both the global one-sided obstacle bound and initial clearance in the specified slab. The sharp corridor result also has a geometry-dependent scale threshold. Blocked use: ['Route past an arbitrary obstacle using the corridor lower bound.', 'Remove the initial clearance or replace the free terminal port by a prescribed terminal without another estimate.']
- **limit-renewal-measures-and-shared-giant** (hypothesis): Stopped renewal-prefix laws are probability measures, while renewal-occurrence measures count weighted prefixes. Paired opposite-end tests integrate the same irreducible shape and charge its weight once. Blocked use: ['Normalize renewal-occurrence counts as though they were stopped-path probabilities.', 'Multiply independent giant probabilities for opposite ends of a shared irreducible.', 'Add a separate horizontal translation sum to the paired integral.']
- **limit-ordered-tests-and-unbiased-failures** (hypothesis): The ordered test includes extremal order in addition to disjointness. Usable-renewal failure estimates are taken before conditioning on continued order. Blocked use: ['Identify ordinary stopped-string disjointness with the ordered test.', 'Apply the usable-renewal failure bound under a future law already conditioned on continued avoidance.']
- **limit-distinct-end-strings-and-height-integration** (hypothesis): Multiplication of turn costs requires distinct end-string variables. Residual-height restrictions must be integrated with bounds uniform in end data before the original probe laws are used. Blocked use: ['Reuse one end string in independently multiplied turn tests.', 'Condition probe laws on completed chain heights or previous survival before applying their estimates.']
- **limit-local-mass-needs-separate-return-control** (hypothesis): A uniform subwalk length bound does not alone control all ball visits. Repeated visits require suppression of many disjoint slab crossings with prescribed endpoints and matching. Blocked use: ['Sum separate ball visits using only one subwalk bound.', 'Apply the many-bridge estimate without its slab-width, strictness, mutual-avoidance, and endpoint-matching conditions.']
- **limit-all-count-cylinder-tail** (hypothesis): The many-crossing argument needs a cylinder tail uniform over every polygon count. An estimate restricted to bounded fugacity does not justify arbitrarily large crossing counts. Blocked use: ['Use bounded-count or bounded-fugacity asymptotics for crossing counts growing with the ambient scale.', 'Change the separating-polygon weight convention by adding a polygon factor.']
- **limit-exact-length-transfer-denominator** (hypothesis): Exact-length probability transfer uses an absolute exceptional critical mass inside a containing full box and the partition-function lower bound at that very length. No independence of subwalks is supplied by the transfer. Blocked use: ['Replace the every-length denominator bound by an averaged normalization.', 'Assume independent subwalks under the uniform fixed-length law.']
- **limit-cover-construction-is-not-optimization** (formal_scope): Temporal blocks give an explicit covering upper-bound construction on the simultaneous good event. This does not certify that the constructed cover minimizes the number of balls. Blocked use: ['Report the temporal-block cover as an optimal cover.', 'Choose block lengths using uncalibrated asymptotic constants at a selected finite length.']
- **limit-no-finite-asymptotic-oracle** (nonconstructive): Finite enumeration or simulation cannot decide the paper's quantified asymptotic probability conclusions. A finite certificate for those conclusions is ABSENT from the supplied corpus. Blocked use: ['Treat finite sample agreement as certification of the asymptotic theorem.', 'Claim executable sampler correctness or computational complexity from polynomial critical-mass bounds.']

          "exact_quote": "<span id=\"thm:main\" label=\"thm:main\"></span>For every $\\delta>0$ and $k>0$, there are constants $C_{\\delta,k}<\\infty$ and $n_0(\\delta,k)$ such that, for every integer $n\\ge n_0(\\delta,k)$, an event of $\\mathbb P_n$-probability at least $1-C_{\\delta,k}n^{-k}$ has all the following properties: $$\\begin{aligned}\n n^{3/4-\\delta}&\\le D(\\gamma)\\le n^{3/4+\\delta},\\label{eq:main-diameter}\\\\\n n^{-\\delta}\\min\\{n,s^{4/3}\\}\n &\\le M_\\gamma(z,s)\\le n^{\\delta}\\min\\{n,s^{4/3}\\},\\label{eq:main-mass}\\\\\n n^{-\\delta}(1+ns^{-4/3})\n &\\le \\mathcal N_\\gamma(s)\\le n^{\\delta}(1+ns^{-4/3}).\\label{eq:main-cover}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The last two bounds hold simultaneously for every $z\\in V(\\gamma)$ and every real $s\\in[1,n]$ (with $z$ relevant only to <a href=\"#eq:main-mass\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:main-mass\">[eq:main-mass]</a>).",
